# Codex D3_HYPOTHESIS_ENGINE Checkpoint Adversarial Review — Round 3

## Round history

- Round 1 (`7368143`) → BLOCK with 9 BLOCK findings RN-01..RN-09 + 2 NIT RN-10/11.
- Round 2 (`388edca`) → BLOCK with 5 BLOCK findings R2-01..R2-05 + 3 NIT R2-06/07/08.

## Round 3 target

- Branch: `feat/r3.0d-d3-hypothesis-engine`
- Round 3 candidate SHA: **`ac9f9a0bb7ab180ba1af5c86bb238473c32b2b02`**
- Train base: `feat/r3.0-integrated-delivery` HEAD = `299771a`
- Round 2 evidence at `governance/r3.0d/codex-checkpoint-evidence/D3/round-2-388edca.raw.txt`

## Round 3 closures

### R2-01 (Correlation strip)
`_validateAuthoritativeGraph` now reconstructs the EXPECTED correlation partition from node
(caseId|sessionId|lapId|sourceId) tuples. Every bucket (singleton or multi-member) MUST map to
exactly one correlationGroup in the graph with matching sorted memberNodeIds and exact-equal
1/count weight. Stripped or fabricated groups are rejected with
`EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT`.

### R2-02 (nodeId grammar + weight NaN)
- Every node.nodeId is validated via `HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_RE, ...)` + `HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_FORBIDDEN_RE, ...)`.
  (`../bad` style IDs → `EVIDENCE_NODE_ID_FORBIDDEN`.) Test cases route through HI, not ambient
  `.test`, so RegExp.prototype.test rebinding cannot defeat the check.
- independenceWeight equality uses `_exactlyEqual(cg.independenceWeight, 1 / cg.memberNodeIds.length)`
  via captured `Object.is`. NaN / Infinity / drift all fail this.

### R2-03 (mandatory freshness)
Step 1.5 resolves reference time. Sources (in order): `opts.referenceNowMs` (finite positive)
→ `opts.clock()` returning ISO 8601 (parsed via captured `Date.parse`). If neither yields a
valid reference, the engine fails-closed with `HYPOTHESIS_INVALID` ("D3 requires a reference
time for mandatory freshness enforcement"). Clock callback invoked AT MOST ONCE; the resolved
ISO is reused for snapshot.createdAt. Per-node freshness must parse; default `DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS`
= 30 days (configurable via `opts.maxAgeMs`). Future-dated freshness (>5min in future) also rejected.

### R2-04 (pre-clone audit removed)
Pre-clone `HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit` was removed. The corrected layered security model:
  1. Frozen-input check (captured `Object.isFrozen`).
  2. `HI.safeStructuredClone` of input.graph — produces a plain non-Proxy tree.
  3. Post-clone `HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit` — catches non-plain smuggling on the plain clone.
  4. D2 invariant re-validation (nodeId grammar, uniqueness, identity scope, lap, imported_summary).
  5. Correlation partition reconstruction + edge endpoint validation.
  6. graphId recompute equality on cloned data — opaque/TOCTOU Proxy whose reads were
     inconsistent fails this check.
HI captures inside the engine are closure-private; they're immune to ambient rebinding
triggered by any Proxy trap during clone. Transparent Proxies (returning identical values
across reads) are accepted because they're functionally equivalent to the underlying graph.

### R2-05 (input/opts accessor TOCTOU)
Engine entry now snapshots `inputIn` and `optsIn` via descriptor-only reads:
- Input: rejects Symbol keys, non-enumerable own keys, accessor descriptors; copies data
  property values into a fresh `HI.safeObjectCreateNull()` bag. `input.graph` is held BY
  REFERENCE (avoiding double-clone that would un-freeze the D2 graph); authority verifier
  does its own structuredClone defang downstream.
- Opts: rejects Symbol keys, non-enumerable, accessors, function fields (except `clock`).
  clock callback held by reference (functions cannot be cloned). Data fields cloned via
  `HI.safeStructuredClone` after descriptor-read assembly into a plain bag.

### R2-06 NIT (test rigor)
Section H3 added with HRR2-01..R2-06 tests. Strict positive contradiction-direction test
HRR2-06-a/b uses an authentic D2-built graph with `contradictingEdges`. HR05-2 + HR05-3
strengthened for default-30-day behavior + no-reference fail-closed.

### R2-07 NIT (capture timing claim narrowed)
Comment narrowed: captures occur after the contract imports load — those loads trust the
module loader; if the module loader is compromised, captures cannot help (out-of-scope).

### R2-08 NIT (manifest stale)
D3.json contractEnforcements rewritten; newSuiteAssertions 87 → 129; evidence files added
to changedFiles.

## Round 3 task

Reproduce every R2 finding and verify closure. Then look for new vectors introduced by R3:

1. **Correlation partition reconstruction**: Is the (caseId|sessionId|lapId|sourceId) key
   construction collision-free under hostile values? Could a hostile graph use special
   characters (e.g. `|`) in identity fields to make two different identities hash to the same
   bucket?
2. **Forbidden ID grammar via routes other than `nodeId`**: Are correlationGroup memberNodeIds
   ALSO grammar-validated? Are edge.from / edge.to grammar-validated? Are the
   sourceId/sourceVersion/lapId strings grammar-validated?
3. **Mandatory freshness ergonomics**: Now that opts.clock() OR opts.referenceNowMs is required,
   is this clearly documented in the contract surface? Is the test ergonomics reasonable
   (callers always pass clock)?
4. **Clock invocation count**: Confirm that opts.clock() is invoked AT MOST ONCE per
   buildHypothesisSet call. Are there any code paths that re-invoke it?
5. **Input snapshot completeness**: With `input.graph` held by reference, can a caller
   provide a NON-frozen input.graph that the snapshot reads (descriptor read sees the
   reference, not the value)? The authority verifier then runs `_isFrozen` on the snapshot's
   `.graph` field — verify this still rejects mutable shells.
6. **Opts snapshot**: Does the opts data-field descriptor read reject hostile patterns:
   accessor on `maxAgeMs`, accessor on `referenceNowMs`, Symbol key, non-enumerable?
7. **Mutation coverage**: The Section J corpus covers J11..J19 ambient rebindings. Are there
   NEW ambient call sites in R3's added code that lack coverage? Specifically:
   - `HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_RE, ...)` and `HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_FORBIDDEN_RE, ...)` —
     covered by J16.
   - Captured `Date.parse` via `_isoToMs` — covered? (Not in J corpus; need a J20.)
   - Captured `Object.is` via `_exactlyEqual` — covered? (Need a J21.)
   - Captured `Object.isFrozen` via `_isFrozenSafe` — partially covered by HR08.
8. **Edge endpoint validation**: Does it check edge.kind is in the closed enum {supports,
   contradicts, derived_from, correlated_with, invalidates}? Currently the code only checks
   non-empty string; an unknown edge.kind could go through.
9. **Correlation partition test rigor**: HRR2-01 strips ONE group — verify the engine rejects.
   What about an EXTRA group with a fake key not derived from any node identity?
10. **Final envelope cap byte calculation**: Was the byte calculation off-by-one or includes
    fields the consumer might not see?

# GitHub

## Overview

Use this skill as the umbrella entrypoint for general GitHub work in this plugin. It should decide whether the task stays in repo and PR triage or should be handed off to a more specific review, CI, or publish workflow.

This plugin is intentionally hybrid:

- Prefer the GitHub app from this plugin for repository, issue, pull request, comment, label, reaction, and PR creation workflows.
- Use local `git` and `gh` only when the connector does not cover the job well, especially for current-branch PR discovery, branch creation, commit and push, `gh auth status`, and GitHub Actions log inspection.
- Keep connector state and local checkout context aligned. If the request is about the current branch, resolve the local repo and branch before acting.

Once the intent is clear, route to the specialist skill immediately and do not keep broad GitHub triage in scope longer than needed.

## Connector-First Responsibilities

Handle these directly in this skill when the request does not need a narrower specialist workflow:

- repository orientation once the repo, PR, issue, or local checkout is identified
- recent PR or issue triage
- PR metadata summaries
- PR patch inspection
- PR comments, labels, and reactions
- issue lookup and summarization
- PR creation after a branch is already pushed

Prefer the GitHub app from this plugin for those flows because it provides structured PR, issue, and review-adjacent data without depending on a local checkout. If the repository is not already identifiable from the user request or local git context, ask for the repo instead of pretending there is a repo-search flow that may not exist.

## Routing Rules

1. Resolve the operating context first:
   - If the user provides a repository, PR number, issue number, or URL, use that.
   - If the request is about "this branch" or "the current PR", resolve local git context and use `gh` only as needed to discover the branch PR.
   - If the repository is still ambiguous after local inspection, ask for the repo identifier.
2. Classify the request before taking action:
   - `repo or PR triage`: summarize PRs, issues, patches, comments, labels, reactions, or repository state
   - `review follow-up`: unresolved review threads, requested changes, or inline review feedback
   - `CI debugging`: failing checks, Actions logs, or CI root-cause analysis
   - `publish changes`: create or switch branches, stage changes, commit, push, and open a draft PR
3. Route to the specialist skill as soon as the category is clear:
   - Review comments and requested changes: `../gh-address-comments/SKILL.md`
   - Failing GitHub Actions checks: `../gh-fix-ci/SKILL.md`
   - Commit, push, and open PR: `../yeet/SKILL.md`
4. Keep the hybrid model consistent after routing:
   - connector first for PR and issue data
   - local `git` and `gh` only for the specific gaps the connector does not cover

## Default Workflow

1. Resolve repository and item scope.
2. Gather structured PR or issue context through the GitHub app from this plugin.
3. Decide whether the task stays in connector-backed triage or needs a specialist skill.
4. Route immediately when the work becomes review follow-up, CI debugging, or publish workflow.
5. End with a clear summary of what was inspected, what changed, and what remains.

## Output Expectations

- For triage requests, return a concise summary of the repository, PR, or issue state and the next likely action.
- For mixed requests, tell the user which specialist path you are taking and why.
- For connector-backed write actions, restate the exact PR, issue, label, or reaction target before applying the change.
- Never imply that GitHub Actions logs are available through the connector alone. That remains a `gh` workflow.

## Examples

- "Use GitHub to summarize the open PRs in this repo and tell me what needs attention."
- "Help with this PR."
- "Review the latest comments on PR 482 and tell me what is actionable."
- "Debug the failing checks on this branch."
- "Commit these changes, push them, and open a draft PR."

    90	  // here means no further mid-call rebinding of THESE references can occur (HI internals are
    91	  // closure-private and themselves cannot be rebound).
    92	  var _HI_API_FROZEN_AT_LOAD = (function () {
    93	    try { return Object.isFrozen(HI); } catch (e) { return false; }
    94	  })();
    95	  var _HI_HAS = (function () {
    96	    var required = [
    97	      'safeOwnKeys', 'safeKeys', 'safeGetOwnDescriptor', 'safeHasOwn',
    98	      'safeArrayPush', 'safeArrayIndexOf', 'safeArrayForEach', 'safeArrayMap',
    99	      'safeArraySlice', 'safeArraySort', 'safeStringSlice', 'safeStringCharCodeAt',
   100	      'safeStringCoerce', 'safeStringToLowerCase', 'safeIsArray', 'safeIsPlainShape',
   101	      'safeNumberIsInteger', 'safeNumberIsFinite', 'safeMathFloor', 'safeRegExpTest',
   102	      'safeUtf8ByteLength', 'safeStructuredClone', 'safeObjectCreateNull',
   103	      'safeObjectAssign', 'safeDefineDataProperty', 'safeGetPrototypeOf',
   104	      'safeGetOwnPropertyNames', 'deepOriginalShapeAudit', 'deepFreeze', 'stableStringify',
   105	    ];
   106	    var ok = true;
   107	    for (var i = 0; i < required.length; i++) {
   108	      if (typeof HI[required[i]] !== 'function') { ok = false; break; }
   109	    }
   110	    return ok;
   111	  })();
   112	
   113	  function _intrinsicsIntact() {
   114	    if (!_HI_API_FROZEN_AT_LOAD) return false;
   115	    if (!_HI_HAS) return false;
   116	    // The HI api itself MUST still be frozen at call time. We use the captured Object.isFrozen
   117	    // (Codex D3 R1 RN-08 closure) so caller cannot defeat this check by rebinding ambient.
   118	    if (!_isFrozenSafe(HI)) return false;
   119	    // Codex D3 R1 RN-10 closure: narrow guard claim. This routine ONLY verifies HI surface
   120	    // integrity (and our captured Object.isFrozen reference is sound, since we capture it before
   121	    // any caller code runs). Validation-path security relies on the HI captured wrappers — the
   122	    // guard is defence-in-depth on the HI surface itself, not a general intrinsic-rebinding
   123	    // detector.
   124	    return true;
   125	  }
   126	
   127	  function _buildIntrinsicTamperBlock(detail) {
   128	    // Build the BLOCK envelope without going through RC.buildBlockedResult (which itself uses
   129	    // intrinsics under the hood). Mirrors evidence-graph.js's defensive posture (D2 Step 17.6).
   130	    var reasonsArr = HI.safeArraySlice([CODES.INTERNAL_CONTRACT_VIOLATION]);
   131	    var explanationsObj = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   132	    var explKey = RC.explanationKeyFor ? RC.explanationKeyFor(CODES.INTERNAL_CONTRACT_VIOLATION) : 'r3.0d.error.internal_contract_violation';
   133	    HI.safeDefineDataProperty(explanationsObj, CODES.INTERNAL_CONTRACT_VIOLATION, explKey);
   134	    var blocked = {
   135	      valid: false,
   136	      reasonCodes: HI.deepFreeze(reasonsArr),
   137	      explanationKeys: HI.deepFreeze(explanationsObj),
   138	      detail: HI.safeStringCoerce(detail || 'intrinsic tampering detected'),
   139	    };
   140	    return HI.deepFreeze(blocked);
   141	  }

   143	  // ---------- Constants -------------------------------------------------------------------------
   144	  var HYPOTHESIS_SET_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1;
   145	  var SERVICE_VERSION = 1;
   146	
   147	  // Caps — generous but bounded. Aligned with D1 hypothesis-contract caps where applicable.
   148	  var HYPOTHESIS_COUNT_CAP = 64;
   149	  var ALT_EXPLANATION_COUNT_CAP = 128;
   150	  var VALIDATION_ACTION_COUNT_CAP = 128;
   151	  var CANNOT_CONCLUDE_CAP = 64;
   152	  var LIMITATIONS_CAP = 64;
   153	  var SUPPORT_ID_PER_HYPOTHESIS_CAP = HC.ID_ARRAY_CAP || 64;
   154	  var CONTRADICT_ID_PER_HYPOTHESIS_CAP = HC.ID_ARRAY_CAP || 64;
   155	  var ALT_ID_PER_HYPOTHESIS_CAP = HC.ALTERNATIVE_ARRAY_CAP || 16;
   156	  var REASON_CODE_PER_HYPOTHESIS_CAP = 32;
   157	  var VAL_ACTION_ID_PER_HYPOTHESIS_CAP = HC.ID_ARRAY_CAP || 64;
   158	  var STRING_BYTE_CAP = HC.STRING_BYTE_CAP || 512;
   159	  var ENVELOPE_BYTE_CAP = 512 * 1024;        // 512 KiB envelope ceiling.
   160	  var GRAPH_NODE_INPUT_CAP = 8192;
   161	  var GRAPH_EDGE_INPUT_CAP = 16384;
   162	  // Codex D3 R2 R2-03 closure: directive §5.18 mandates staleness-based invalidation.
   163	  // Default cutoff is 30 days. Caller can override via opts.maxAgeMs; reference time MUST
   164	  // be supplied via opts.clock() returning ISO 8601 (preferred) or opts.referenceNowMs.
   165	  var DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;
   166	
   167	  // Confidence state ladder (semantic primary; numeric score is auxiliary).
   168	  var CONFIDENCE_STATES = HI.deepFreeze(['not_computed', 'insufficient_evidence', 'low', 'moderate', 'high']);
   169	  // Status ladder.
   170	  var HYPOTHESIS_STATUSES = HI.deepFreeze(['supported', 'contradicted', 'inconclusive', 'blocked']);
   171	
   172	  // Confidence score bounds — integer 0..100. Avoids float ambiguity entirely.
   173	  var SCORE_MIN = 0;
   174	  var SCORE_MAX = 100;
   175	
   176	  // Conclusion credibility ladder (per credibility-contract).
   177	  var CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY_ORDER = HI.deepFreeze(['Physics', 'Model', 'Measured', 'Derived', 'Heuristic', 'Unavailable']);
   178	  function _crIndex(c) { return HI.safeArrayIndexOf(CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY_ORDER, c); }
   179	  function _crAtLeast(a, b) {
   180	    // a, b in CONCLUSION_CREDIBILITY_ORDER. lower index = stronger. a is at-least-as-strong as b iff a's index ≤ b's index.
   181	    var ia = _crIndex(a); var ib = _crIndex(b);
   182	    if (ia === -1 || ib === -1) return false;
   183	    return ia <= ib;
   184	  }
   185	
   186	  // ID grammar mirrors D1 hypothesis-contract.
   187	  var ID_RE = /^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$/;
   188	  var ID_FORBIDDEN_RE = /(\.\.|[\/\\]|^\.)/;
   189	
   190	  // ---------- Helper utilities (HI-only) --------------------------------------------------------
   191	  function _isNonEmptyString(v) {
   192	    return typeof v === 'string' && v.length > 0;
   193	  }
   194	  function _isFiniteNumber(v) {
   195	    return typeof v === 'number' && HI.safeNumberIsFinite(v) === true;
   196	  }
   197	  function _isInteger(v) {
   198	    return HI.safeNumberIsInteger(v) === true;
   199	  }
   200	  function _isPlainObject(v) {
   201	    return HI.safeIsPlainShape(v) === 'plain-object';
   202	  }
   203	  function _isPlainArray(v) {
   204	    return HI.safeIsPlainShape(v) === 'plain-array';
   205	  }
   206	  function _isFrozen(v) { return _isFrozenSafe(v); }
   207	  function _byteLength(s) {
   208	    if (typeof s !== 'string') return 0;
   209	    return HI.safeUtf8ByteLength(s);
   210	  }
   211	  function _strcmp(a, b) {
   212	    if (a < b) return -1;
   213	    if (a > b) return 1;
   214	    return 0;
   215	  }
   216	  function _arrPush(arr, v) {
   217	    HI.safeArrayPush(arr, v);
   218	  }
   219	  function _setHas(setObj, key) {
   220	    var d = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(setObj, key);
   221	    return !!(d && d.value === true);
   222	  }
   223	  function _setAdd(setObj, key) {
   224	    HI.safeDefineDataProperty(setObj, key, true);
   225	  }
   226	  function _newSet() {
   227	    return HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   228	  }
   229	  // Hash function MUST be byte-equivalent to D2 evidence-graph.js _hash64 (FNV-1a 32-bit twice,
   230	  // concatenated: hash32(s) + hash32(s + '|' + s.length)). This is the only way an authoritative
   231	  // graphId recompute on the D3 side can match the D2-produced value. Re-using BigInt FNV-1a 64-bit
   232	  // would produce a different hash and reject every authentic graph as "forged".
   233	  function _toHex8(h) {
   234	    var hexChars = '0123456789abcdef';
   235	    var out = '';
   236	    var x = h >>> 0;
   237	    for (var i = 7; i >= 0; i--) {
   238	      var nibble = (x >>> (i * 4)) & 0xF;
   239	      out += hexChars[nibble];
   240	    }
   241	    return out;
   242	  }
   243	  function _hash32(s) {
   244	    var h = 0x811c9dc5;
   245	    var len = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   246	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   247	      h ^= HI.safeStringCharCodeAt(s, i);
   248	      h = (h + ((h << 1) + (h << 4) + (h << 7) + (h << 8) + (h << 24))) >>> 0;
   249	    }
   250	    return _toHex8(h);
   251	  }
   252	  function _hashFNV64Hex(s) {
   253	    var slen = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   254	    return _hash32(s) + _hash32(s + '|' + slen);
   255	  }
   256	  // ---------- Authoritative graph verification --------------------------------------------------
   257	  // The graph payload that produced graphId in D2 (see r3-0d-evidence-graph.js Step 16, ~line
   258	  // 1308): { caseAssociation, nodes (projected), edges, correlationGroups (projected) }. To verify
   259	  // authority, we project the same fields out of the suspect graph and recompute the hash with
   260	  // the same prefix ('graphid|v1|'). Mismatch → fabrication.
   260	  // the same prefix ('graphid|v1|'). Mismatch → fabrication.
   261	  function _projectIdPayload(graph) {
   262	    var projectedNodes = HI.safeArrayMap(graph.nodes, function (n) {
   263	      var idPart = {
   264	        caseId: n.identity.caseId,
   265	        sessionId: n.identity.sessionId,
   266	        lapId: n.identity.lapId == null ? null : n.identity.lapId,
   267	        sourceId: n.identity.sourceId,
   268	        sourceVersion: n.identity.sourceVersion,
   269	      };
   270	      var obsPart = {
   271	        kind: n.observation.kind,
   272	        channel: n.observation.channel == null ? null : n.observation.channel,
   273	        i18nKey: n.observation.i18nKey,
   274	        params: n.observation.params == null ? null : n.observation.params,
   275	      };
   276	      return {
   277	        nodeId: n.nodeId,
   278	        category: n.category,
   279	        credibility: n.credibility,
   280	        provenance: n.provenance,
   281	        availability: n.availability,
   282	        confidence: { state: (n.confidence && n.confidence.state) ? n.confidence.state : null },
   283	        identity: idPart,
   284	        observation: obsPart,
   285	        limitations: n.limitations,
   286	        supportingEdges: n.supportingEdges,
   287	        contradictingEdges: n.contradictingEdges,
   288	      };
   289	    });
   290	    var projectedCgs = HI.safeArrayMap(graph.correlationGroups, function (g) {
   291	      return {
   292	        correlationGroupId: g.correlationGroupId,
   293	        memberNodeIds: g.memberNodeIds,
   294	        independenceWeight: g.independenceWeight,
   295	      };
   296	    });
   297	    return {
   298	      caseAssociation: {
   299	        caseId: graph.caseAssociation.caseId,
   300	        sessionId: graph.caseAssociation.sessionId,
   301	        lapId: graph.caseAssociation.lapId == null ? null : graph.caseAssociation.lapId,
   302	      },
   303	      nodes: projectedNodes,
   304	      edges: graph.edges,
   305	      correlationGroups: projectedCgs,
   306	    };
   307	  }
   308	
   309	  function _recomputeGraphId(graph) {
   310	    var idPayload = _projectIdPayload(graph);
   311	    var serialized = HI.stableStringify(idPayload);
   312	    return 'graph_' + _hashFNV64Hex('graphid|v' + graph.schemaVersion + '|' + serialized);
   313	  }
   314	
   315	  /**
   316	   * Validate that the supplied object IS a D2 authoritative graph snapshot. Returns
   317	   * { valid:true } or { valid:false, reasonCodes:[...] }.
   318	   *
   319	   * Checks (in order):
   320	   *  - top-level plain-object + deep-frozen
   321	   *  - schemaVersion 1
   322	   *  - required top-level keys present
   323	   *  - graphId starts with 'graph_' + 16 hex
   324	   *  - caseAssociation valid
   325	   *  - sessionAssociation.sessionId === caseAssociation.sessionId
   326	   *  - nodes/edges/correlationGroups are frozen arrays
   327	   *  - node/edge caps not exceeded
   328	   *  - recomputed graphId === asserted graphId
   329	   *  - cross-case: every node.identity.caseId === graph.caseAssociation.caseId
   330	   *  - cross-session: every node.identity.sessionId === graph.caseAssociation.sessionId
   331	   */
   332	  function _validateAuthoritativeGraph(gIn, refNowMs, maxAgeMs) {
   333	    if (!_isPlainObject(gIn)) {
   334	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   335	    }
   336	    // Codex D3 R2 R2-04 closure: do NOT do a pre-clone audit. HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit
   337	    // invokes Proxy traps (getPrototypeOf / ownKeys / etc) which is itself the attack surface
   338	    // we wanted to avoid. We instead rely on the layered security model:
   339	    //   1. structuredClone produces a brand-new plain-object tree. The clone process invokes
   340	    //      Proxy [[Get]] traps, but the result is concrete data — no Proxy survives.
   341	    //   2. The clone is then audited via deepOriginalShapeAudit (clone never has Proxy → audit
   342	    //      is meaningful here, not a no-op trap walk).
   343	    //   3. The graphId is recomputed from the cloned data. A TOCTOU/opaque Proxy that returns
   344	    //      inconsistent values across the clone reads will yield a cloned tree whose
   345	    //      recomputed hash does NOT match the asserted graphId → rejected.
   346	    //   4. The frozen-input requirement (captured Object.isFrozen) rejects mutable shells.
   347	    // HI captures within the engine are themselves closure-private and immune to ambient
   348	    // rebinding triggered by Proxy traps. The combination is functionally Proxy-safe.
   349	    if (!_isFrozen(gIn)) {
   350	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   351	    }
   352	    var g = HI.safeStructuredClone(gIn);
   353	    if (g === null) {
   354	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   355	    }
   356	    if (!_isPlainObject(g)) {
   357	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   358	    }
   359	    // Post-clone audit — the clone IS plain (no Proxy), so this is meaningful: catches
   360	    // class-instance / Array-subclass / Symbol-key / accessor / sparse / non-finite-number
   361	    // smuggling through structuredClone's permissive deep-clone semantics.
   362	    if (HI.deepOriginalShapeAudit(g) !== true) {
   363	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   364	    }
   365	    if (g.schemaVersion !== 1) {
   366	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.UNSUPPORTED_FUTURE_SCHEMA] };
   367	    }
   368	    if (!_isNonEmptyString(g.graphId)) {
   369	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   370	    }
   371	    if (g.graphId.length > 64 || !HI.safeRegExpTest(/^graph_[0-9a-f]{16}$/, g.graphId)) {
   372	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   373	    }
   374	    if (!_isPlainObject(g.caseAssociation)) {
   375	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   376	    }
   377	    if (!_isNonEmptyString(g.caseAssociation.caseId) || !_isNonEmptyString(g.caseAssociation.sessionId)) {
   378	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   379	    }
   380	    if (!_isPlainObject(g.sessionAssociation)
   381	        || g.sessionAssociation.sessionId !== g.caseAssociation.sessionId) {
   382	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_SESSION_MISMATCH] };
   383	    }
   384	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.nodes)) {
   385	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   386	    }
   387	    if (g.nodes.length > GRAPH_NODE_INPUT_CAP) {
   388	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.GRAPH_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   389	    }
   390	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.edges)) {
   391	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   392	    }
   393	    if (g.edges.length > GRAPH_EDGE_INPUT_CAP) {
   394	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.GRAPH_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   395	    }
   396	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.correlationGroups)) {
   397	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   398	    }
   399	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.limitations)) {
   400	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   401	    }
   402	    if (!_isPlainArray(g.cannotConclude)) {
   403	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   404	    }
   405	
   406	    // Per-node identity scope + D2 invariant re-validation. Codex D3 R1/R2 closures:
   407	    // the graphId hash is a diagnostic identifier, not an integrity proof; D3 MUST independently
   408	    // re-enforce every D2 invariant on the snapshot it has just cloned. We do NOT trust the
   409	    // hashed projection alone — the projection itself is not the full graph schema.
   410	    var caseId = g.caseAssociation.caseId;
   411	    var sessionId = g.caseAssociation.sessionId;
   412	    var caseLapId = (g.caseAssociation.lapId == null) ? null : g.caseAssociation.lapId;
   413	    var seenNodeIds = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   414	    var nodeIdSet = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   415	    // Build the expected correlation partition from node identities. Two nodes share a
   416	    // correlation group iff they share (caseId|sessionId|lapId|sourceId). This mirrors D2's
   417	    // _correlationGroupKey logic. Codex D3 R2 R2-01 closure: D3 must reconstruct and verify the
   418	    // partition itself, not just validate the listed groups in isolation.
   419	    var expectedGroupBuckets = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();  // key → { memberNodeIds: [...] }
   420	    var nodeCorrelationKey = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();    // nodeId → key
   421	
   422	    for (var i = 0; i < g.nodes.length; i++) {
   423	      var n = g.nodes[i];
   424	      if (!_isPlainObject(n)) {
   425	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   426	      }
   427	      if (!_isPlainObject(n.identity)) {
   428	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_INVALID] };
   429	      }
   430	      // Codex D3 R2 R2-02 closure: enforce ID_RE grammar + forbidden pattern + byte cap on
   431	      // every node.nodeId. _isNonEmptyString alone admitted '../bad' style IDs.
   432	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.nodeId)) {
   433	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_MISSING_ID] };
   434	      }
   435	      if (HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_FORBIDDEN_RE, n.nodeId) || !HI.safeRegExpTest(ID_RE, n.nodeId)) {
   436	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_ID_FORBIDDEN] };
   437	      }
   438	      if (_byteLength(n.nodeId) > STRING_BYTE_CAP) {
   439	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.BYTE_CAP_EXCEEDED] };
   440	      }
   441	      // Codex D3 R1 RN-02 closure: D2 invariant — no duplicate nodeId.
   442	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(seenNodeIds, n.nodeId)) {
   443	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_DUPLICATE_ID] };
   444	      }
   445	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(seenNodeIds, n.nodeId, true);
   446	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(nodeIdSet, n.nodeId, true);
   447	
   448	      if (n.identity.caseId !== caseId) {
   449	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_CASE_MISMATCH] };
   450	      }
   451	      if (n.identity.sessionId !== sessionId) {
   452	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_SESSION_MISMATCH] };
   453	      }
   454	      var nodeLapId = (n.identity.lapId == null) ? null : n.identity.lapId;
   455	      if (caseLapId !== null && nodeLapId !== null && caseLapId !== nodeLapId) {
   456	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.SOURCE_IDENTITY_LAP_MISMATCH] };
   457	      }
   458	      if (n.identity.sourceId === 'imported_summary' && n.credibility === 'measured') {
   459	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_IMPORTED_SUMMARY_ELEVATION_FORBIDDEN] };
   460	      }
   461	      if (!_isNonEmptyString(n.identity.freshness)) {
   462	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   463	      }
   464	      var freshMs = _isoToMs(n.identity.freshness);
   465	      if (freshMs === null) {
   466	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   467	      }
   468	      // Codex D3 R2 R2-03 closure: freshness enforcement is now MANDATORY. refNowMs must be
   469	      // available (resolved from opts.clock or opts.referenceNowMs). maxAgeMs has a default
   470	      // (DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MS). If no reference time is available we fail-closed before this
   471	      // function is even called — see buildHypothesisSet entry.
   472	      if ((refNowMs - freshMs) > maxAgeMs) {
   473	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   474	      }
   475	      if (freshMs > (refNowMs + (5 * 60 * 1000))) {
   476	        // Reject future-dated freshness (>5min in the future) — clock-skew tolerance only.
   477	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_FRESHNESS_STALE] };
   478	      }
   479	
   480	      // Build expected correlation group bucket for this node.
   481	      var corrKey = caseId + '|' + sessionId + '|' + (nodeLapId == null ? ' ' : nodeLapId) + '|' + n.identity.sourceId;
   482	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(nodeCorrelationKey, n.nodeId, corrKey);
   483	      var bucketDesc = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, corrKey);
   484	      if (bucketDesc) {
   485	        _arrPush(bucketDesc.value, n.nodeId);
   486	      } else {
   487	        HI.safeDefineDataProperty(expectedGroupBuckets, corrKey, [n.nodeId]);
   488	      }
   489	    }
   490	
   491	    // Codex D3 R2 R2-01 closure: validate that the graph's correlationGroups EXACTLY match the
   492	    // expected partition derived from node identities. EVERY bucket (singleton or multi-member)
   493	    // MUST appear as exactly one correlationGroup with the same memberNodeIds (sorted) and
   494	    // independenceWeight = exact 1 / count (Object.is equality — Codex D3 R2 R2-02 closure
   495	    // rejects NaN/Infinity). This mirrors D2's behavior: D2 emits one correlation group per
   496	    // distinct (case|session|lap|sourceId) identity tuple.
   497	    var expectedKeys = HI.safeOwnKeys(expectedGroupBuckets) || [];
   498	    var expectedAllKeys = [];
   499	    for (var eki = 0; eki < expectedKeys.length; eki++) {
   500	      var ek = expectedKeys[eki];
   501	      var dEK = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, ek);
   502	      if (dEK && _isPlainArray(dEK.value) && dEK.value.length >= 1) {
   503	        _arrPush(expectedAllKeys, ek);
   504	        HI.safeArraySort(dEK.value, _strcmp);
   505	      }
   506	    }
   507	    HI.safeArraySort(expectedAllKeys, _strcmp);
   508	    if (g.correlationGroups.length !== expectedAllKeys.length) {
   509	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   510	    }
   511	    var matchedKey = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   512	    var globalMemberSeen = HI.safeObjectCreateNull();
   513	    for (var cgi = 0; cgi < g.correlationGroups.length; cgi++) {
   514	      var cg = g.correlationGroups[cgi];
   515	      if (!_isPlainObject(cg) || !_isPlainArray(cg.memberNodeIds) || cg.memberNodeIds.length === 0) {
   516	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   517	      }
   518	      // All member IDs share the same expected correlation key.
   519	      var firstMember = cg.memberNodeIds[0];
   520	      var keyDesc = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeCorrelationKey, firstMember);
   521	      if (!keyDesc) {
   522	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   523	      }
   524	      var keyForThisGroup = keyDesc.value;
   525	      var bucketDescForKey = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(expectedGroupBuckets, keyForThisGroup);
   526	      if (!bucketDescForKey) {
   527	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   528	      }
   529	      var expectedMembers = bucketDescForKey.value;
   530	      if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(matchedKey, keyForThisGroup)) {
   531	        // The same correlation key appears twice in correlationGroups — invalid.
   532	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   533	      }
   534	      HI.safeDefineDataProperty(matchedKey, keyForThisGroup, true);
   535	      if (cg.memberNodeIds.length !== expectedMembers.length) {
   536	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   537	      }
   538	      // Verify members are exactly the expected set (sorted equality).
   539	      var sortedActual = HI.safeArraySlice(cg.memberNodeIds);
   540	      HI.safeArraySort(sortedActual, _strcmp);
   541	      for (var mi = 0; mi < sortedActual.length; mi++) {
   542	        if (sortedActual[mi] !== expectedMembers[mi]) {
   543	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   544	        }
   545	        if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, sortedActual[mi])) {
   546	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   547	        }
   548	        if (HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(globalMemberSeen, sortedActual[mi])) {
   549	          return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   550	        }
   551	        HI.safeDefineDataProperty(globalMemberSeen, sortedActual[mi], true);
   552	      }
   553	      // Exact Object.is(weight, 1/count) — rejects NaN, Infinity, or any float drift.
   554	      if (!_exactlyEqual(cg.independenceWeight, 1 / cg.memberNodeIds.length)) {
   555	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_CORRELATED_METRICS_DOUBLECOUNT] };
   556	      }
   557	    }
   558	
   559	    // Edge endpoint validation: every edge.from and edge.to must reference a known nodeId.
   560	    for (var ei = 0; ei < g.edges.length; ei++) {
   561	      var e = g.edges[ei];
   562	      if (!_isPlainObject(e) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.from) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.to) || !_isNonEmptyString(e.kind)) {
   563	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   564	      }
   565	      if (!HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, e.from) || !HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(nodeIdSet, e.to)) {
   566	        return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_GRAPH_ORPHAN] };
   567	      }
   568	    }
   569	
   570	    // Recompute graphId — independent authority verification. NOTE: the graphId only hashes
   571	    // (caseAssociation, nodes-projection, edges, correlationGroups). Other fields like
   572	    // graph.cannotConclude / graph.limitations / graph.provenance / graph.createdAt are NOT
   573	    // bound to graphId — Codex D3 R1 RN-01 closure: D3's output must NOT consume those fields
   574	    // from the input graph (they are re-derived from rule firing below).
   575	    var recomputed;
   576	    try { recomputed = _recomputeGraphId(g); }
   577	    catch (e) { return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] }; }
   578	    if (recomputed !== g.graphId) {
   579	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED] };
   580	    }
   581	
   582	    // Deep-freeze the cloned snapshot before returning so downstream code receives an
   583	    // immutable view (the clone was mutable until now).
   584	    return { valid: true, graph: HI.deepFreeze(g) };
   585	  }
   586	
   587	  // ---------- Rule registry (closed) ------------------------------------------------------------
   588	  // Each rule deterministically tests against the sanitized graph and may produce zero or one
   589	  // hypothesis. New rules require new module load; runtime registration is NOT supported.
   590	  function _frozenRule(o) { return HI.deepFreeze(o); }
